# GXemul on GitHub and other public git hosts: forks, derivatives and their fixes, checked against GXEMUL-SEC

Research report, 2026-10-22. Scope: GitHub, GitLab.com, Codeberg, SourceHut, Gitee and Debian Salsa repositories of GXemul (mirrors, forks, derivatives) and their issues and pull requests. Every CRASH/BOUNDS and CORRECTNESS change found was cross-checked against our hardened fork's source, `C:\DocumentNoSnc\cc\GXEMUL\GXEMUL-SEC\src\`, at HEAD `de8c7547` (working tree read on 2026-10-22). The work was read-only on the project: nothing under `C:\DocumentNoSnc\cc\GXEMUL` was written, built or run. Clones live in `C:\Users\User00\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--DocumentNoSnc-cc-GXEMUL\8e474f41-4d47-4c99-bf11-3406a84c4fcd\scratchpad\ghresearch\clones\`.

## Summary

* **Repositories.** 46 repositories carry GXemul source or patches: 43 on GitHub (including ours, `indigoidk/gxemul-0.7.0-hardened`, and NetBSD's pkgsrc tree), 1 on Codeberg and 2 on Debian Salsa. 22 of them carry code changes of their own; the rest are pure mirrors, identical forks, or packaging. About 230 further repositories only mention or use GXemul (OS course labs, LLVM-m88k, recipes, Docker wrappers). GitLab.com, SourceHut and Gitee returned nothing.
* **Most valuable sources**, in order:
  1. https://github.com/rpajarola/gxemul — 80 commits since 2026-10-06 on 0.7.0, mostly DECstation fidelity.
  2. https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860 — 497 commits on 2014 SVN, PowerPC and PReP devices.
  3. **The upstream SVN tail after the 0.7.0 tarball** — 44 commits through 2021-08-30 plus r6577 (2023-04-10), mirrored by retroprom and Randrianasulu.
  4. https://github.com/jroark/GXemul — 77 commits on 0.7.0: MIPS CP0 timing, VR4131.
  5. https://github.com/ajfa/mvme187-sysv88-gxemul — a 2026-09 recipe carrying 8 source patches for the MVME187/88100.
  6. https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul and https://github.com/ryoon/gxemul (pkgsrc patches).
* **Candidate changes.** 367 table rows, one per commit or per independent part of a commit, across seven per-source tables (part (b)).
  - ALREADY FIXED in our fork: 59.
  - NOT PRESENT (code differs, or fixed upstream before 0.7.0): 44.
  - STILL OPEN, in whole or part: 145.
  - UNCERTAIN: 3.
  - FEATURE/BUILD/OTHER (n/a): 119.
* **STILL OPEN detail.** About 27 of the 145 still-open rows map to rows already filed in `OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md` / the ledger; about 118 are not filed (per grep of `OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md` for each). After merging 4 cross-source duplicates, that is about 141 distinct defects. They range from one guest-reachable host crash, through guest-reachable emulator halts and silent wrong results, to device-fidelity gaps.
* **Counts:** derived by `C:\Users\User00\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--DocumentNoSnc-cc-GXEMUL\8e474f41-4d47-4c99-bf11-3406a84c4fcd\scratchpad\tally_status.py` over the seven tables. Each row's status is keyed on the first status keyword in its "Status in our fork" cell, and STILL OPEN is counted anywhere in the cell. The filed/unfiled split is a text heuristic, so treat it as approximate.

**Top 5 (detail in part (c)):**

1. **88100 scaled `xmem.bu rD,rS1[rS2]` calls a NULL function pointer on the host.** Found while reading the ajfa MVME187 patches.
2. **MIPS primary opcodes with no decoder case halt the emulator from user mode:** JALX, LWC2/LDC2/SWC2/SDC2, SWC3 and others. Three independent sources point at this.
3. **The X11 cursor is not clipped to the visible framebuffer.** A guest can make the host over-read the heap (rpajarola `fe69c7b`).
4. **PowerPC: `crandc`/`crorc` compute NAND/NOR, and several legal instructions halt emulation:** `twi`/`tw`, `lswx`/`stswx`, `crnand`, the OE=1 forms and `dcbi` (prozacchiwawa).
5. **MIPS FPU RECIP/RSQRT/MOVCF/MOVZ/MOVN.fmt are undecoded,** giving an endless Coprocessor-Unusable re-trap plus a host log flood.

**Two records corrections** (part (c)):
* Our "unmodified upstream baseline" commit `39748e3c` already contains fork fixes `#9` and `#13`.
* `OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md:1896-1898` rejected upstream's m88k PFAR fix for a reason that is false.

**One host-exit defect** came up while reading. No fork fixes it and it is unfiled: the VR41xx 1 KB-page TLB write `exit(1)`.

## How the statuses were decided

* **The baseline used for "upstream 0.7.0".** It is SVN commit `743170f0` (2021-04-22, "Preparing for a quick 0.7.0 release..."). Its `src/` matches the release tarball byte for byte. The tarball import in `jroark/GXemul`, `rpajarola/gxemul`, `eddieh/gxemu-sdl` and Debian's `e4b5b4d` is the same. **Our `39748e3c` differs from it in exactly two non-generated files**, and both are our own fixes:
  - `configure` carries the `-fgnu89-inline` probe (`#9`).
  - `src/cpus/generate_arm_r.c` reads `1U << (c-1)` (`#13`).

  So every commit in `retroprom/gxemul` `743170f0..HEAD`, and r6577, is upstream work our baseline lacks.
* **Status definitions:**
  - **ALREADY FIXED:** the defect is in upstream 0.7.0 and our fork has fixed it (our file:line and round cited).
  - **NOT PRESENT:** the code differs. Either upstream fixed it before 0.7.0, or the code or feature does not exist in 0.7.0.
  - **STILL OPEN:** the defect is present in our source today (our file:line cited, plus the filed row id if one exists).
  - **UNCERTAIN:** reading the source cannot settle it; the row says what would.

  Every status comes from reading the function in our fork. Line numbers come from `grep -n` or a read of the file.
* **Seven parallel read-only research agents** each took one source family. The load-bearing claims in the top recommendations were re-verified by reading before this report was written:
  - the NULL table entries behind the m88k `xmem` crash;
  - the missing MIPS primary-opcode cases and the halt in `bad:`;
  - the cursor clamp versus the X image size;
  - `crandc`/`crorc` against Book I;
  - the RECIP/RSQRT decode gap;
  - the VR41xx `exit(1)`;
  - the mb89352 format string;
  - the SH reserved-opcode path.

  Nothing was compiled or run, so every status is a source reading, not a measurement. The exceptions are rpajarola row 16 (the debugger Makefile dependency, measured in a scratch replica) and the baseline comparisons (measured with `diff`).
* **Out of scope here.** Distribution patch sets (pkgsrc, Debian, FreeBSD, Gentoo, nixpkgs) are covered by a separate distro-patch report (`B_distro_patches.md` in the same scratch folder). pkgsrc's and Salsa's current patch lists appear in part (a) only as pointers, because the same changes reach GitHub through the Codeberg, skrll and Randrianasulu repositories and are cross-checked there.

## (a) Repositories found

**How they were found.**
* GitHub repository search, run with and without forks, over these terms: `gxemul`, `gavare`, `in:name`, `in:description`, `in:readme`, `topic:gxemul` and `mips64emul`. That gave 62 hits by name/description and 270 by README.
* `git ls-remote` on every hit. Equal HEADs identify pure mirrors.
* Each fork family's remotes were fetched into one clone and `git rev-list ... --not <parent>` was used to count unique commits.
* API searches on GitLab.com, Codeberg, SourceHut, Gitee and Debian Salsa, plus a web search.

"Own commits" means commits not reachable from the repository's upstream import or parent, measured on the clones on 2026-10-22. "Last commit" is the newest commit date on any branch.

## (c) Recommendations, ranked by value

**How these are ranked.** In order of weight:
1. Severity: host crash, then emulator halt, then silent wrong results, then fidelity.
2. Who can reach it: user mode, kernel, or configuration.
3. How cheap the fix is.
4. Whether the claim was re-verified by reading.

All file:line references are to `GXEMUL-SEC/src/` at HEAD `de8c7547`. Nothing below has a runtime witness yet; each item says what was verified by reading.

### 1. 88100 scaled byte `xmem` makes the host call a NULL function pointer

**Status:** CRASH/BOUNDS, STILL OPEN, unfiled.

**The defect.**
- The register-form decoder sends every `xmem` with rD ≠ r0 to `X(xmem_slow)` (`cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:3651-3657`).
- There, `scaled = iword & 0x200` and `size = iword & 0x400`; size is 0 for `xmem.bu` (`:2357-2358`).
- It indexes `m88k_loadstore[]` with `M88K_LOADSTORE_SCALEDNESS` (`:2377-2382`), then calls the result unconditionally: `xmem_load(cpu, &call)` at `:2395`.
- The generator emits `NULL` for every `size == 0 && scaledness` entry (`cpus/generate_m88k_loadstore.c:104-105`, `:254-257`).

**Who can reach it.** `xmem` without `.usr` is unprivileged, so any user process on luna88k, mvme88k or testm88k can crash the host process with one instruction. The 88100 manual defines `xmem.bu rD,rS1[rS2]` with a scale factor of 1 (`_scratchpad/mc88100.txt:6423`, `:6459`).

**Same table gap, smaller effect.** Scaled `ld.b` / `ld.bu` / `st.b` decode to `ic->f = NULL`. That triggers "INTERNAL ERROR: ic->f == NULL" and the CPU stops (`cpu_dyntrans.c:1906-1908`).

**Source.** ajfa/mvme187-sysv88-gxemul, patch 01 of https://github.com/ajfa/mvme187-sysv88-gxemul/commit/4a01173afc4beafabbd61ca385bd158d07d4a415. It makes scaled byte ld/st unscaled, but exempts xmem (`op != 3`), so it leaves the crash in place. The xmem case was derived while reading that patch (part (b), A7 rows 29-30).

**Fix.** Treat a scaled byte access as unscaled (the manual's scale of 1). Doing it in the table generator covers ld, st and xmem at once. About one line, plus a detector row.

**Verified:** by reading, twice (the research agent, then re-read for this report).

### 2. MIPS primary opcodes with no decoder case halt the emulator, from user mode

**Status:** CRASH/BOUNDS (emulator stop), STILL OPEN, unfiled. The filed sibling `mipscop0tail` covers only the COP0 arms.

**The defect.**
- `cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:5708` is `default:goto bad;`.
- `bad:` in `cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c:1983-2014` prints "UNIMPLEMENTED instruction" and sets `cpu->running = 0`.
- Opcodes with no case: 0x1d JALX, 0x32 LWC2, 0x36 LDC2, 0x3a SWC2, 0x3b SWC3, 0x3e SDC2.
- Same shape nearby: 0x1e on non-R5900 (`:5564-5581`), and LWC3 below ISA IV (`:5552-5562`).

**Who can reach it.** Any guest code, at any privilege level, on every MIPS machine, including the pmax and arc rigs.

**Sources** (three independent):
- https://github.com/klx3300/gxemul-hos/commit/366d5cc51b807b311a3bb26e07851b42e651806f changes `default: goto bad` to `instr(reserved)`.
- https://github.com/jroark/GXemul/commit/3c3d5cbfa23ce58718d6c1c348f44587b35c0911 implements JALX.
- The CHERI-family review found the same gap.

**Fix.** Use the idiom of `#236` / `#237` / `#309`: a print-once note, then the architected exception, keeping `goto bad` only for `translation_readahead`.
- CpU(2) for LWC2/LDC2/SWC2/SDC2. Our HI6_COP2 arm already raises CpU for COP2 operations (`:5122-5133`).
- RI for JALX (Config.M16 = 0, `cpu_mips_coproc.c:61`) and for 0x1e.

**Verified:** by reading (no case arms; `bad:` sets `running = 0`).

### 3. X11 cursor is not clipped to the visible framebuffer: guest-driven host heap over-read

**Status:** CRASH/BOUNDS, STILL OPEN, unfiled.

**The defect.**
- `devices/dev_fb.c:244-252` clamps the cursor against `d->xsize` / `d->ysize`, which are the VRAM dimensions.
- The X image is created at the visible size: `d->x11_xsize = d->visible_xsize / scaledown` (`:913-914`), used at `console/x11.c:635-637`.
- PMAG-BA is 1024x864 visible inside 1024x1024 VRAM (`devices/dev_turbochannel.c:250-251`). So a guest BT459 cursor Y between 865 and (1024 − cursor height) passes the clamp.
- `XSubImage()` at `console/x11.c:247-251` then starts below the 864-row image. According to the research agent's reading of libX11, `_XSubImage` reads rows past the buffer.

**Who can reach it.** Only builds with `WITH_X11`, run with `-X`.

**Source.** https://github.com/rpajarola/gxemul/commit/fe69c7bb763d9012ccafbe689cc3617d1684655e clips the sub-image to the image and draws nothing when the cursor is fully off-screen.

**Fix.** About 20 lines. Also clamp against the visible size in `dev_fb_setcursor`.

**Verified:** the clamp/visible-size mismatch was verified by reading. The libX11 over-read was not run.

### 4. PowerPC: wrong results from `crandc` / `crorc`, and legal instructions that halt emulation

**Status:** CORRECTNESS + CRASH/BOUNDS (emulator stop), STILL OPEN, unfiled.

**Wrong results.**
- `X(crandc)` sets BT when `!(ba & bb)`, which is NAND (`cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c:2716-2724`).
- `X(crorc)` sets BT when `!(ba | bb)`, which is NOR (`:2743-2751`).
- Book I defines them as `CR[BT] ← CR[BA] & ¬CR[BB]` and `CR[BA] | ¬CR[BB]` (`_scratchpad/ppc_uisa_book1_v201.txt:1903-1914`).
- These are silently wrong condition results in compiled code. Neither name appears in CHANGELOG or OUTSTANDING_BUGS.
- Each fix is one line. Source: prozacchiwawa [241f5e2](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/241f5e273caf75599ebb8ebc554e1e9faaacf508) and [0685c8d](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/0685c8da85ded843eb8aae952ae0769e0b1b8648).

**Emulator stops on legal instructions.**
- `twi` (primary 3 → `:5426` `goto bad`). Linux/ppc MMIO read accessors issue `twi 0,rX,0`.
- `tw`, `lswx`/`stswx`, and `dcbi` (all reach the opcode-31 default at `:5172`).
- `crnand` (`:4531`).
- The OE=1 add/subf forms and `addc.` (`:5062-5137`).

**Sources.** prozacchiwawa commits listed in part (b), A4 rows 7-11. Use Book I as the spec rather than the fork's handlers: its OE set is incomplete, and its CA rewrite introduces bugs (A4 row 27, "do not port").

**Verified:** `crandc`/`crorc` by reading against Book I. The decode gaps by the research agent's reading.

### 5. MIPS FPU: RECIP/RSQRT and MOVCF/MOVZ/MOVN.fmt are undecoded, causing an endless re-trap and a host log flood

**Status:** CORRECTNESS / hang, STILL OPEN, unfiled.

**The defect.**
- `fpu_function()` returns 0 for these functions (`cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:1798`).
- The dispatcher then prints an ungated `fatal("UNIMPLEMENTED coproc1 function")` and raises CpU(1) with CU1 already set (`:2585-2589`). That is the endless re-trap `#295` fixed for round/ceil/floor, plus one host log line per execution.
- RECIP (0x15) and RSQRT (0x16) are MIPS IV / MIPS64 / MIPS32r2 instructions (`mips64_volII.txt:1396`, `:1429`; `mips-iv.txt:1541`). MOVCF/MOVZ/MOVN.fmt are 0x11-0x13 (`mips64_volII.txt:1209`, `:1221`, `:1245`).

**Who can reach it.** Guests on R5000 / RM5200 / R10000 / R12000 / 5Kc-class CPUs (`include/mips_cpu_types.h:75-103`).

**Source.** https://github.com/TheFausap/gxemul/commit/380649a10c6c6934494e92d21891c5f5dd450b93 shows the idea only: its arithmetic is never decoded.

**Related gaps (not from any fork):**
- COP1X (opcode 0x13: MADD/MSUB/NMADD/NMSUB.fmt, LWXC1/SDXC1, PREFX) always raises CpU(3) (`cpu_mips_instr.c:5136-5142`).
- SPECIAL MOVCI (movf/movt) falls to `goto bad` (`:4679`).

**Verified:** by reading.

### Runners-up

6. **SMP: invalidate translated code on every CPU.**
   - Source: upstream [55eea37f](https://github.com/retroprom/gxemul/commit/55eea37f) + [6d83f6da](https://github.com/retroprom/gxemul/commit/6d83f6da).
   - Ours invalidates only on the writing CPU (`cpus/memory_rw.c:757-760`), so `-n`>1 guests can run stale translations.
   - Prerequisite: first move the three stores out of `*_cpu_new` (`cpu_mips.c:303`, `cpu_arm.c:187`, `cpu_alpha.c:101`), or NULL-check them. Without that, upstream's own loop segfaults (skrll `a4e2335`).
   - Still partial afterwards (`cpu_dyntrans.c:825-827`).
7. **ns16550 IIR should report one prioritised interrupt ID.**
   - Fixed independently by two forks: jroark [2a1a395](https://github.com/jroark/GXemul/commit/2a1a395a693cbcd2f635d88b9204842c88d6b902) and prozacchiwawa [f58d6d7](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/f58d6d745800f300eecab01e97d8630c15e20daf).
   - Ours ORs the flag bits, so RX plus THRE pending reads 0x6, which is the "receiver line status" ID; every IIR read also clears THRE (`devices/dev_ns16550.c:83-104`, `:212-213`).
   - This UART model is shared by many machines.
8. **Small MIPS devices can be hidden by a RAM mapping.**
   - Source: rpajarola [e37235e](https://github.com/rpajarola/gxemul/commit/e37235e61de104fc7402793e6bba9fdd7aeb2607).
   - The KN02 RTC (0x100 bytes) and DZ (0x80 bytes) sit in memory_rw's MIPS PROM-area range, which falls through to RAM (`memory_rw.c:662-667`, `:738-750`; `dev_mc146818.c:671-682`).
   - One guest store just past the registers maps the whole page as RAM and silently disconnects the device.
9. **88100 legal encodings that halt the CPU.**
   - Register-form `sub` / `add.c*` / `sub.c*`, opcodes 0x71-0x77 (`cpu_m88k_instr.c:3660-3684` → `:3815`).
   - `xmem` with instruction word 0 (`:3047-3048`).
   - The `.wt` bit (`:3606-3614`).
   - Also: PSR/EPSR reserved bits are not masked (`cpu_m88k.c:553`, `:557`; the manual allows only bits 31-28 and 3-0 to change).
   - Sources: upstream r6577 (sub) and the ajfa patch 01.
10. **88100 bus errors should set PFSR/PFAR.**
    - Sources: upstream [4e3e72b1](https://github.com/retroprom/gxemul/commit/4e3e72b1) + r6577(e) + [6cb3514c](https://github.com/retroprom/gxemul/commit/6cb3514c).
    - Our sites: `memory_rw.c:614-623`, `:662-712`; `dev_luna88k.c:624-627`.
    - Needed by `lunadefaultstop`'s fault-based fix; also corrects a wrong record (below).
11. **8259 PIC.**
    - EOI clears the IRR bit of a still-asserted line (`dev_8259.c:107`, `:122`).
    - ICW1 SNGL/IC4 are ignored, so the guest's first mask write is swallowed (`:183-206`).
    - ISR is never set, so read-ISR always returns 0.
    - Source: prozacchiwawa [ae2240b](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/ae2240b30a994913d56f904533ee64d0178682ee) and [598ab10](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/598ab108cacb56dfbeae33eb463fd2b8c18393dd).
12. **NAT: a failed outgoing connect looks like a successful connect followed by a close.**
    - Our sites: `net/net_ip.c:594-601`, `:1520-1552`.
    - Source: rpajarola [1c18c2b](https://github.com/rpajarola/gxemul/commit/1c18c2b1ddb90a62cd27c4873b436511d730383d).
13. **DC7085 serial chip.**
    - CTS is missing on lines 2/3, which hangs the OSF/1 console (`dev_dc7085.c:261-262`). Source: rpajarola [807d80b](https://github.com/rpajarola/gxemul/commit/807d80bf5be49ec5ef4d2f590904bea23bd11966).
    - An early return while TRDY is set starves receive (RDONE), so Mach 3 keyboard input is ignored (`:149-150`). Source: the ajfa mach3 patch.
14. **`debugger.o` is not rebuilt when `debugger_cmds.c` changes.**
    - Our site: `debugger/Makefile.skel:13`. Source: rpajarola [3e8f2fd](https://github.com/rpajarola/gxemul/commit/3e8f2fd70a9a96478bff1dc7fee61cc0e6872123).
    - **Measured** with GNU Make 4.4.1 in a scratch replica. This is a stale-binary hazard for this project's own binary-freshness rule.
15. **mb89352 format string has three conversions but only two arguments.**
    - Our site: `dev_mb89352.c:305-309`. Source: upstream [8f4c717f](https://github.com/retroprom/gxemul/commit/8f4c717f).
    - Undefined behaviour a guest can trigger; fold it into the open `qhmbunimplstops` work.

### Adjacent finding: a host exit no fork fixes

* **VR41xx TLB write with a 1 KB page calls `exit(1)`.**
  - When a VR4100-class TLB write installs a valid entry with PageMask 0 (a legal 1 KB page on VR41xx), the emulator runs `fatal("1KB pages don't work with dyntrans.\n"); exit(1);`. The sites are `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:2037-2042` (old entry) and `:2165-2170` (new entry).
  - R4100 PageMask writes skip `#188`'s canonicalisation (`:748-750`).
  - Result: a host process exit reachable by guest kernel code on hpcmips. Unfiled; verified by reading. It is in the same class as this fork's other host-kill rows and probably deserves filing first.

### Records corrections found along the way

1. **`39748e3c` ("Import GXemul 0.7.0 (unmodified upstream baseline)") is not unmodified.**
   - Compared with the release tree (SVN `743170f0`, which equals jroark `c35c056`, rpajarola `09d4f62`, eddieh's base and Debian's `e4b5b4d`), it differs in exactly two non-generated files:
     - `configure`: the `-fgnu89-inline` probe, our `#9`.
     - `src/cpus/generate_arm_r.c`: `1U << (c-1)`, our `#13`.
   - It also carries generated files (`tmp_*.c`, `autodev.c`, `automachine.c`, fonts, `ppc_spr_strings.h`) and two `grep.exe.stackdump` files.
   - `gate_ab` builds its "pristine" tree from `git archive 39748e3`, so that tree includes `#9` and `#13`. The effect is harmless (identical generator output), but the commit message is a wrong record.
   - Measured by `diff` four times, independently (this report and three research agents).
2. **`OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md:1896-1898` rejects upstream's m88k PFAR fix (`6cb3514c`) with the reason "PFAR appears nowhere in src/". That is false.** `memory_m88k.c:399`, `:405` write PFAR, and `dev_m8820x.c:437-438` let the guest read it, so the defect is live (part (b), A1 row 20).

The result value is STILL OPEN: ours gives LO=0, and `ddiv` gives HI=dividend, while the manual's pseudocode gives the wrapped quotient with HI=0 (part (b), A6 row 5). |
| https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul/pull/2 , https://github.com/rmn30/gxemul/pull/1 | Optional "Count +1 on every read" and no-interrupts-while-stepping; no auto-tracing in the debugger. | NOT PRESENT as a defect. Since SVN `2cbe517` (2010) the read increments are undone at run boundaries (`cpu_dyntrans.c:417-428`), so reads cannot push Count past Compare and lose the interrupt. |

(b) UNCERTAIN: undiagnosed. |

## (b-1) Index of STILL OPEN items, deduplicated and grouped by severity

This index lists the 145 still-open rows of part (b-2), deduplicated to about 141 distinct defects. Cross-source duplicates are merged on one line.

How to read each line:
- **Defect** comes first.
- **Our site** is relative to `GXEMUL-SEC/src/` at HEAD `de8c7547`.
- **Filed** gives the row id when one exists in `OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md` or the ledger; otherwise it says "unfiled".
- **Rows** point into part (b-2): `A4-14` means table A4, row 14. The source commit links are in those rows.

### Class 1: host crash or undefined behaviour in the host process
- **88100 scaled `xmem.bu` calls a NULL function pointer.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:2357-2395`; `cpus/generate_m88k_loadstore.c:104-105`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-30.
- **X11 cursor sub-image not clipped to the visible framebuffer** (host heap over-read). Our site: `console/x11.c:247-251`; `devices/dev_fb.c:244-252`. Unfiled. Rows: A2-12.
- **ns16550 divisor divide-by-zero in a debug() argument** (fires at -O0/-O1). Our site: `devices/dev_ns16550.c:179-181`. Filed: `ns16550`, carried by `closeassessrows`. Rows: A5-1.
- **mb89352 message format has three conversions, two arguments.** Our site: `devices/dev_mb89352.c:305-309`. Unfiled. Rows: A1-22.
- **`single_step_breakpoint` declared with two incompatible types** (`volatile int` definition vs `extern int` declarations). Our site: `debugger/debugger.c:73` vs `cpus/generate_head.c:119`. Unfiled (latent). Rows: A1-11.
- **`bool running` registered as a UINT8 setting** (debugger-only). Our site: `cpus/cpu.c:93`. Unfiled. Rows: A1-17.
- **lswi signed-overflow shifts** (`0xff << 24`, `d << 24`). Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c:3056-3059`. Unfiled. Rows: noted in A4-17.

### Class 2: host process exit or abort on guest input
- **Footbridge SDRAM_BA_MASK/OFFSET reads call `exit(1)`.** Our site: `devices/dev_footbridge.c:732-736`, `:757-761`. Filed: `fbsdrampair` (HIGH). Rows: A6-28.
- **PVR "no zwrite" calls `exit(1)`.** Our site: `devices/dev_pvr.c:1243-1246`. Filed: `pvrrenderexit`. Rows: A1-49.
- **Alpha DEC 3000/300 `irq=0` string makes construction call `abort()`.** Our site: `machines/machine_alpha.c:69-72`. Filed: `ctorabortclass`. Rows: A1-53.
- **PPC failed slow-path instruction fetch calls `exit(1)`.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c:4115-4121`. Filed: `qhslowifetch`. Rows: A4-1.
- **osiop SCRIPTS features call `exit(1)`** (block MOVE forms, WAIT RESELECT, register ops 5-7, JUMP carry/data, MEMORY MOVE, register default). Our site: `devices/dev_osiop.c:404-427`, `:714-715`, `:725-727`, `:817-830`, `:866-870`, `:1143-1152`. Filed: `exitunowned76` (+ site ids). Rows: A7-38.
- **clmpcc STCR / default arm call `exit(1)`.** Our site: `devices/dev_clmpcc.c:161-164`, `:246-252`. Filed: `exitunowned76`. Rows: A7-42.
- **MVME187 MEMC register read out of bounds; `exit(1)` on a memconf write or the default arm.** Our site: `devices/dev_mvme187.c:68`, `:80`, `:90`. Filed: `mvme187memcoob`, `exitunowned76`. Rows: A7-49.
- **VR41xx 1 KB-page TLB write calls `exit(1)`** (adjacent finding, no fork fixes it). Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:2037-2042`, `:2165-2170`. Unfiled. Rows: part (c).

**MIPS**
- **Primary opcodes with no decoder case** (JALX, LWC2, LDC2, SWC2, SWC3, SDC2, 0x1e, LWC3 below ISA IV). Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:5708`. Unfiled. Rows: A3-13, A7-3.
- **Unknown COP0 function raises RI where user mode should get CpU**, plus an unlatched fatal() on every translation. Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:4930-4938`. Filed in part: `mipscop0tail`. Rows: A2-64.

**PowerPC**
- **`twi` / `tw` undecoded.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c:5426`, `:5172`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-7.
- **`lswx` / `stswx` undecoded.** Our site: `:5172`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-8.
- **`crnand` undecoded.** Our site: `:4531`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-9.
- **OE=1 XO forms and `addc.` undecoded.** Our site: `:5062-5137`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-10.
- **`dcbi` undecoded.** Our site: `:5172`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-11.
- **Update-form load/store with RA=0 halts.** Our site: `:4338-4342`, `:4995-4999`. Unfiled (low). Rows: A4-12.
- **MSR[IP]=1 "reboot hack" stops the emulator.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc.c:288-292`. Filed: `qhppciphack`. Rows: A4-2.
- **`fcmpo` undecoded.** Our site: `:5283`. Filed: `qhppcdecode`. Rows: A4-38.

**PC devices**
- **8259 OCW2/OCW3 commands outside the short list halt.** Our site: `devices/dev_8259.c:138-141`. Filed: `8259cmdhalt`. Rows: A5-2.

**88100 CPU**
- **Register-form `sub` / `add.c*` / `sub.c*` (0x71-0x77) undecoded.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:3660-3684`, `:3815`. Unfiled. Rows: A1-57, A7-25.
- **`xmem` with instruction word 0 halts.** Our site: `:3047-3048`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-28.
- **Scaled `ld.b` / `ld.bu` / `st.b` decode to a NULL `ic->f` and stop.** Our site: `:3608-3629`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-29.
- **`.wt` bit halts.** Our site: `:3606-3614`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-31.
- **PSR.MXM halts.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_m88k.c:542-547`. Filed: `m88kmxmkill`. Rows: A1-50.

**luna88k devices**
- **mb89352 non-byte access and SCMD 0x24 halt.** Our site: `devices/dev_mb89352.c:304-315`, `:364`, `:527-535`. Filed: `qhmbunimplstops`. Rows: A1-24.
- **mb89352 manual `running = 0` sites**, plus SCMD decode, BUS FREE interrupt and SDGC. Our site: `dev_mb89352.c:146,169,176,235,242,290,312,533,690`; `:364`; `:107`; `:562-569`. Filed: the halts (`qhm88kstops`, `qhmbphasestops`, `qhmbbufendstops`, `qhmbunimplstops`); decode/BFINT/SDGC unfiled. Rows: A1-27.
- **luna88k default arm halts, and `RESET_CPU0..3` reach it.** Our site: `devices/dev_luna88k.c:909-924`. Filed: `lunadefaultstop`, `lunafuse`. Rows: A1-29.

**Dreamcast**
- **BIOS `GDROM_ABORT_COMMAND` (r7=8) halts.** Our site: `promemul/dreamcast.c:303-322`, `:382-388`. Unfiled. Rows: A1-52.

### Class 4: guest hang, livelock, unbounded counter, or a console/serial dead end
- **MIPS FPU RECIP/RSQRT/MOVCF/MOVZ/MOVN.fmt: CpU(1) re-trap plus an ungated log flood.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:1798`, `:2585-2589`. Unfiled. Rows: A6-15.
- **Console `select()` retried forever on errors such as EBADF.** Our site: `console/console.c:279-294`. Filed: `qhselectretry`. Rows: A3-5.
- **MIPS Compare backlog is an uncapped `int`, and the host-timer rate is off by x2.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:422`, `:808-809`. Filed in part: `dfreq`. Rows: A3-26.
- **MIPS Compare delivered from host SIGALRM when `emulated_hz` > 0.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:796-820`. Filed: `n1`, `dfreq`. Rows: A3-27.
- **VR41xx RTCL1: period taken from the low half only, high half ignored, backlog uncapped.** Our site: `devices/dev_vr41xx.c:604-615`, `:396`. Filed in part: `rtcnarrow` → `closeassessrows`. Rows: A3-17.
- **3MIN SCC DMA is under `#if 0`, so the Ultrix serial console waits forever.** Our site: `devices/dev_dec_ioasic.c:180-258`. Unfiled. Rows: A2-47.
- **DC7085 reports no CTS on lines 2/3, which hangs the OSF/1 console.** Our site: `devices/dev_dc7085.c:261-262`. Unfiled. Rows: A2-60.
- **DC7085 TRDY early return starves RDONE (Mach 3 keyboard).** Our site: `devices/dev_dc7085.c:149-150`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-52.
- **SYNC is not gated to MIPS II+** (an R3000 must raise RI; Mach 3's user emulator spins). Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:4675-4677`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-53.
- **pckbc Set-LEDs parameter byte is NAKed with RESEND.** Our site: `devices/dev_pckbc.c:820-823`. Unfiled. Rows: A5-12.
- **pckbc 0xA9 queues nothing; 0xAA / 0xFF do not flush the queues.** Our site: `devices/dev_pckbc.c:1056-1061`, `:883-900`. Unfiled. Rows: A5-13.

### Class 5: silent wrong results in CPU or MMU emulation

**PowerPC**
- **`crandc` computes NAND; `crorc` computes NOR.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c:2716-2724`, `:2743-2751`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-14, A4-15.
- **DS-form XO bits ignored: ldu/lwa/stdu run as ld/std at EA+1/+2** (64-bit CPUs; PPC970 is the testppc/bareppc default). Our site: `:4295`, `:4277-4320`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-13.
- **slw/srw/sraw shift amounts 32..63.** Our site: `:3133-3161`. Filed: `ppcshiftwide`. Rows: A4-16.
- **lswi does not zero-fill the unfilled bytes of the last register.** Our site: `:3056-3059`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-17.
- **Interrupt entry leaves IR/DR/FP/SE/BE/FE0/FE1/POW set, and LE is not taken from ILE.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc.c:335`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-18.
- **ISI does not set its SRR1 reason bits.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc.c:327`; `cpus/memory_ppc.c:315-323`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-19.
- **PTE referenced/changed (R/C) bits are never set.** Our site: `cpus/memory_ppc.c:131-232`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-20.
- **tlbie invalidates only the exact EA, not across segments.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c:3930-3935`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-21.
- **A change of MSR IR/DR does not flush the EA-keyed translation cache.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc.c:265-312`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-22.
- **BAT writes leave stale translations.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c:2807-2810`. Filed: `ppcbatstale`. Rows: A4-23.
- **mtdec flipping bit 0 from 0 to 1 does not signal the decrementer.** Our site: `:2807-2810`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-24.
- **mftb increments TBL on every read.** Our site: `:2789-2795`. Filed: `ppctblcarry`. Rows: A4-25.
- **SPRG2 writes are discarded on 32-bit CPUs.** Our site: `:2811-2818`. Unfiled. Rows: A4-26.
- **lwarx/stwcx. DSI records a stale SRR0 (no PC sync).** Our site: `:2276-2375`. Filed: `ppcllscsrr0`. Rows: A4-4.
- **FPSCR: FPRF class, FR/FI/XX/OX/UX and enabled-exception delivery.** Filed: `ppcfprfclass`, `ppcfpstatus`, `ppcenabldeliv`. Rows: A4-39.

**MIPS**
- **DIV/DDIV INT_MIN/-1 result** (LO should be the wrapped quotient; ddiv returns HI = dividend). Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:1839-1840`, `:1863-1867`. Unfiled. Rows: A6-5.
- **FCSR IEEE cause/flag bits not set.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:1354`. Filed: `qhfcsrflags`. Rows: A2-42.
- **MIPS I R2010/R3010 FPU interrupt (IP5) is unwired.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips.c:258`; `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:1093-1095`. Known gap, no row id. Rows: A2-43.
- **An MFC0 Count bump that reaches Compare does not raise IP7.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:590-595`. Unfiled. Rows: A3-28.
- **Count advances once per instruction rather than every second cycle**; no per-machine divisor. Our site: `cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c:425-426`. Unfiled. Rows: A3-29, A3-30.
- **Count does not advance during WAIT idle batches.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c:376-377`. Unfiled. Rows: A3-31.
- **R4100 Context.BadVPN2 should be VA[31:11].** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips.c:2078-2088`. Filed: `mipsbadvpnscale`. Rows: A3-32.
- **VR4131 HIBERNATE wakes at the next instruction instead of through the reset vector.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:4892-4915`. Unfiled. Rows: A3-24.
- **EBase (CP0 15 select 1) missing for the modelled Release-2 cores.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:892-894`; `cpus/cpu_mips.c:2165-2168`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-2.

**SMP and memory**
- **Translated code is invalidated only on the writing CPU.** Our site: `cpus/memory_rw.c:757-760`. Unfiled. Rows: A1-33.
- **PROM-emulation helper exceptions are discarded by `X(promemul)`.** Our site: `core/memory.c:695-939`; `cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:2653-2656`. Unfiled. Rows: A5-33.
- **memory_rw's device arm warms translations under NO_EXCEPTIONS.** Our site: `cpus/memory_rw.c:526-581`. Recorded only as a mechanism note. Rows: A5-34.
- **Sub-page MIPS devices can be shadowed by a RAM mapping** (KN02 RTC, DZ). Our site: `devices/dev_mc146818.c:671-682`; `cpus/memory_rw.c:662-667`, `:738-750`. Unfiled. Rows: A2-66.

**88100 CPU and devices**
- **PSR/EPSR reserved bits are not masked.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_m88k.c:553`, `:557`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-24.
- **Bus errors do not set PFSR/PFAR.** Our site: `cpus/memory_rw.c:614-623`, `:662-712`. Recorded only inside `lunadefaultstop`. Rows: A1-25.
- **luna88k 0x3ffffff0 probe does not set PFAR** (our records rejected this fix for a false reason). Our site: `devices/dev_luna88k.c:624-627`. Unfiled. Rows: A1-20.
- **Accesses beyond RAM should raise a bus error.** Our site: `cpus/memory_rw.c:663-713`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-33.
- **osiop DMA writes do not invalidate translated code.** Our site: `devices/dev_osiop.c:262-277`. Unfiled. Rows: A7-37.

**SuperH**
- **User-mode access to P1/P2/P4 raises a TLB miss instead of an address error.** Our site: `cpus/memory_sh.c:359-370`. Filed: `shuseraddr`. Rows: A6-33.

### Class 6: device fidelity

**DECstation** (mostly rpajarola)

*LANCE*
- **Station-address ROM alias and checksum.** Our site: `dev_le.c:955-956`, `:1027`, `:1086-1093`. Rows: A2-8.
- **STOP/STRT semantics.** Our site: `:862-877`, `:803-812`. Rows: A2-9.
- **Received FCS bytes left stale.** Our site: `:598`. Rows: A2-10.
- **No loopback mode.** Our site: `:438`. Rows: A2-11.
- **CSR1-3 implemented-bit masks.** Our site: `:884-886`. Rows: A2-26.

*bt459 framebuffer*
- **Retrace interrupt rate, port mirror, cursor colours, comparator.** Our site: `dev_bt459.c:574`, `:576`, `:386-388`. Rows: A2-13.

*53C94 SCSI (asc)*
- **CFG1 forced to SCSI ID 7.** Our site: `dev_asc.c:1135-1136`. Rows: A2-22.
- **Select-with-ATN message bytes from DMA.** Our site: `:909`, `:987-997`. Rows: A2-63.
- **Bare "WARNING!!!!!!!!! BUG!!!!" printf.** Our site: `:519-522`. Recorded (OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md:2345). Rows: A2-48.

*3MIN and IOASIC*
- **3MIN SCSI DMA through the IOASIC missing.** Our site: `machine_pmax.c:309-310`; `dev_dec_ioasic.c:83-90`. Rows: A2-23.
- **IOASIC_INTR write semantics.** Our site: `dev_dec_ioasic.c:261-265`. Rows: A2-25.
- **IOASIC station address is synthetic.** Our site: `:415`. Rows: A2-27.

*Z8530 SCC*
- **WR0 register pointer and commands.** Our site: `dev_scc.c:402-405`. Rows: A2-29.
- **Interrupt-pending semantics (four defects).** Our site: `:379-388`, `:432-435`, `:215-216`, `:201`/`:454`. Rows: A2-30.
- **Loopback routed to the keyboard line.** Our site: `:447-451`, `:282-285`. Rows: A2-31.
- **WR9 reset, Send Abort, underrun latch, BRG zero count.** Rows: A2-32.
- **3MIN serial line mapping.** Our site: `:445`. Rows: A2-33.

*MC146818 RTC*
- **Register 63 (DEC NVRAM) overwritten with the year.** Our site: `dev_mc146818.c:258`, `:272`, `:529`. Rows: A2-34.
- **A guest-set time reverts to host time.** Our site: `:459-463`, `:536-540`. Rows: A2-35, A5-29.
- **UF/UIE update interrupt.** Our site: `:357-379`. Rows: A2-36.

*TURBOchannel and PROM*
- **Empty-slot reads give no bus error.** Our site: `dev_turbochannel.c:143-149`. Rows: A2-45.
- **3MIN option slot numbers (cosmetic).** Our site: `machine_pmax.c:330`, `:338`. Rows: A2-46.
- **DEC PROM `systype` and jump-table close() (Ultrix 3.1D).** Our site: `machine_pmax.c:935-940`; `dec_prom.c:186-195`. Rows: A2-65.

**SCSI and disk**
- **Raw tape returns a spurious zero record before each filemark; a short final record is not reported.** Our site: `diskimage_scsicmd.c:36-41`, `:805`; `diskimage.c:1347-1357`, `:1627-1628`. Rows: A2-5.
- **CD-ROM capacity rounded up to whole cylinders.** Our site: `diskimage.c:302-303`. Rows: A2-56.
- **MODE SELECT accepts only NetBSD's header; unlatched fatal() hex dump otherwise.** Our site: `diskimage_scsicmd.c:1306-1337`. Rows: A2-61.
- **INQUIRY allocation length forced to at least 36.** Our site: `:370-376`. Filed: `b120r`. Rows: A5-16.
- **INQUIRY EVPD ignored.** Our site: `:363-367`. Rows: A5-17.
- **MODE SENSE page control ignored; unlatched fatal().** Our site: `:591-593`. Rows: A5-19.
- **WRITE AND VERIFY(10) and RESERVE/RELEASE fall to the default arm.** Our site: `:1380-1384`. Filed: `b120`. Rows: A5-20, A5-21.

**PC / PReP chipset and UART**
- **ns16550 IIR should report one prioritised ID** (two forks fixed it independently). Our site: `dev_ns16550.c:83-104`, `:212-213`. Rows: A3-34, A5-11.
- **8259 EOI clears the IRR bit of a still-asserted line.** Our site: `dev_8259.c:107`, `:122`. Rows: A5-25.
- **8259 ICW1 SNGL/IC4 ignored.** Our site: `:183-206`. Rows: A5-26.
- **8259 ISR never set; PReP IACK has no acknowledge side effect.** Our site: `:108`; `dev_prep.c:52-62`. Rows: A5-27.
- **pccmos index not masked to 7 bits.** Our site: `dev_pccmos.c:74`. Rows: A5-28.
- **bus_pci CONFIG_ADDRESS keeps the low bits; unlatched fatal().** Our site: `bus_pci.c:77-82`. Rows: A5-30.
- **Eagle 0xCFD-0xCFF sub-dword config access.** Our site: `dev_eagle.c:65-76`. Rows: A5-31.
- **MPC105 memory-controller registers.** Our site: `bus_pci.c:1336-1346`. Rows: A5-32.
- **pckbc keyboard ID answers AB 41 where AB 83 is due.** Our site: `dev_pckbc.c:848-861`. Rows: A5-14.
- **pckbc 0xD4 aux write echoed into the keyboard stream.** Our site: `:1077-1085`, `:792-807`. Rows: A5-15.

**MVME187 / 88k devices** (mostly the ajfa recipe)
- **osiop CALL does not save the return address in TEMP.** Our site: `dev_osiop.c:859`, `:810`. Rows: A7-39.
- **osiop ISTAT SIGP, CTEST2, selection timeout (STO), SFBR.** Our site: `:1063-1083`, `:1043-1051`, `:695-700`. Rows: A7-40.
- **osiop wide accesses not split into byte lanes.** Our site: `:1155-1167`. Rows: A7-41.
- **osiop SCRIPTS from on-board SRAM or never-written RAM.** Filed: `osiopzeroram`. Rows: A7-36.
- **clmpcc MSVR CD/DSR/CTS, IACK context, EOI.** Our site: `dev_clmpcc.c:175-177`, `:226-234`. Rows: A7-43.
- **mk48txx multi-byte NVRAM access.** Our site: `dev_mk48txx.c:84-85`, `:121`. Rows: A7-47.
- **mk48txx clock does not run.** Our site: `:165`, `:129-135`. Rows: A7-48.

**SH-4**
- **SCIF FDR receive count clamped at 255 instead of 16.** Our site: `dev_sh4.c:2819-2824`. Filed: `qhscifssr`. Rows: A6-30.

**VR41xx / hpcmips** (jroark)
- **VR4131 SIU registered `in_use=1`, so it races the console for stdin.** Our site: `dev_vr41xx.c:777-781`. Rows: A3-11.
- **BE-300 framebuffer declared 15 bpp instead of 16.** Our site: `machine_hpcmips.c:80`. Rows: A3-12.
- **VR4131 register map (ETIME/ECMP/RTCL1/GIU offsets).** Our site: `dev_vr41xx.c:580-645`. Rows: A3-15.
- **GIUINTSTAT write-one-clear at +0x148/+0x14a.** Our site: `:545-549`, `:617-621`, `:637-645`. Rows: A3-16.

**Network**
- **NAT: a failed outgoing connect looks connected.** Our site: `net_ip.c:594-601`, `:1520-1552`. Rows: A2-53.
- **DHCP reply lacks option 53 and puts the server address in giaddr.** Our site: `net_ip.c:1074`, `:1109-1110`, `:1123-1139`. Rows: A3-38.
- **RARP reply can assign 10.0.0.0 when q == 0.** Our site: `net.c:250-254`. Rows: A2-59.

**Console**
- **X11 keycode table:** arrow keys send nothing under evdev, and some F-key sequences are wrong. Our site: `x11.c:839-900`. Rows: A2-20.

### Class 7: log floods, build, hygiene, fidelity flags
- **One fatal() line per PPC exception and per translation fault.** Our site: `cpus/cpu_ppc.c:329-331`; `cpus/memory_ppc.c:302-305`. Belongs to the `floodclass` family, unfiled. Rows: A1-10.
- **Two fatal() lines per ARM abort.** Our site: `cpus/memory_arm.c:261-266`. Mentioned inside `armvecrecurse`. Rows: A1-41.
- **`debugger.o` not rebuilt after an edit to `debugger_cmds.c`** (measured). Our site: `debugger/Makefile.skel:13`. Unfiled. Rows: A2-16.
- **4Kc/4KEc/5Kc/5KE are modelled with an FPU** (pkgsrc flagged it; do not port the hunk as-is). Our site: `include/mips_cpu_types.h:101-104`. Unfiled. Rows: A6-37.

## (b-2) Full tables: one row per candidate change

The seven tables below are the complete per-source catalogues, copied verbatim. Each was produced by one read-only research agent working on one source family.

Every row has the same columns: source commit URL, files/function, description, category, status in our fork, and evidence.

Line numbers refer to `GXEMUL-SEC/src/` at HEAD `de8c7547`, as read on 2026-10-22.

Each table keeps its own scope notes and its own "top picks". The cross-source ranking is in part (c), and the deduplicated still-open list is in part (b-1).

Status counts, from `tally_status.py`:

| Table | Rows | ALREADY FIXED | NOT PRESENT | STILL OPEN (in whole or part) | UNCERTAIN | n/a |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 upstream tail | 66 | 15 | 10 | 16 | 0 | 25 |
| A2 rpajarola | 67 | 12 | 1 | 37 | 0 | 18 |
| A3 jroark | 42 | 6 | 4 | 17 | 1 | 14 |
| A4 rs6000 PowerPC | 46 | 9 | 7 | 25 | 0 | 5 |
| A5 rs6000 devices | 50 | 7 | 6 | 22 | 0 | 15 |
| A6 CHERI + pkgsrc | 41 | 2 | 15 | 6 | 1 | 17 |
| A7 small/older + ajfa | 55 | 8 | 1 | 22 | 1 | 25 |
| **Total** | **367** | **59** | **44** | **145** | **3** | **119** |

Notes on the counts:
- Three rows are counted under ALREADY FIXED by their first keyword but also contain a STILL OPEN part: A2 row 64, and A7 rows 36 and 41. That is why the STILL OPEN column here adds up to 145, while a count by first keyword alone gives 142, and why those two tables' columns sum to more than their row counts.
- In A4, row 3 is ALREADY FIXED, but its residual `exit(1)` is filed separately as `ppcllscrcexit`.

### A1: the upstream SVN tail after 0.7.0 (retroprom, Randrianasulu r6577, skrll, Codeberg, eventhorizon5)

Source table: `A1_upstream_tail.md`.

#### A1 — The upstream SVN tail after 0.7.0, checked against GXEMUL-SEC

**Scope.** I covered all 55 commits in `retroprom/gxemul` `743170f0..HEAD`: the 44 that touch `src/` or `configure`, plus 11 that change only docs or tests (one grouped row). I also covered SVN r6577 (`Randrianasulu/GXemul` `9bfc91e`), skrll's four commits, Codeberg `user3412` (`040d951`, `7112e6d`), the Debian TAP patch (`8d461a7`) and `eventhorizon5` `10b3102`.

**Line numbers.** All `file:line` citations point into `GXEMUL-SEC/src/` at HEAD `de8c7547`. None of the cited files has uncommitted edits in the working tree. "upstream070" means the pristine 0.7.0 copy.

##### Cross-cutting findings (read these first)

1. **Upstream's breakpoint refactor (`6d2d8bf0`) made every `VERBOSITY_ERROR` debugmsg halt the CPU by default.** It prints "Aborting." and sets `running = false` (r6577 `core/debugmsg.c:268-285`, `:604`). As a result, most of upstream's later "debugmsg-ifying" changes turn a host `exit(1)` into a CPU halt rather than letting the guest continue. That applies to `0e39b6de`, `d72d9d4a` and `8300465f`, and to `a1cafe7c`/`d72d9d4a` removing their manual `running = 0` lines.
   - In our fork an ERROR message does not halt: break-on-ERROR is opt-in (`#261`, `core/debugmsg.c:155-159`).
   - So the explicit `running = 0` lines in our tree are the halts, and porting upstream's text alone does not decide what our fork should do at those sites. We still need our own choice there.
2. **skrll's `arm_cpu_new` segfault (`a4e2335`) only exists after `55eea37f`.** That commit loops over `machine->cpus[]` while the CPU array is still being filled. Our tree has no such loop.
   - Exactly three `*_cpu_new` functions write to memory: `cpu_mips.c:303`, `cpu_arm.c:187`, `cpu_alpha.c:101`.
   - These stores must be moved out of `*_cpu_new` (or the loop must NULL-check each CPU) before the SMP fix can be ported.
3. **One of our existing records is wrong.** `OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md:1896-1898` rejects upstream's "m88k PFAR" fix because "PFAR appears nowhere in src/".
   - That reason is false: since the 0.7.0 import (`39748e3c`), `memory_m88k.c:399,405` writes PFAR and `dev_m8820x.c:437-438` lets the guest read it.
   - The defect upstream `6cb3514c` fixes is therefore still live (row 20).

Also fixes that check's own printf strings (leading dash) and retries the compiler test with `-std=c99`. | BUILD | n/a | None of the three is in our `configure` (`:711` is still 0.7.0's math-library check). ##### Top picks (still open, worth porting)

1. **Row 33 (`55eea37f` + `6d83f6da`): invalidate translated code on all CPUs.**
   - This is the only fix in the tail that changes emulation correctness in shared code. Any guest run with `-n`>1 can execute stale translated code today.
   - Port it only together with moving the three stores out of `*_cpu_new` (rows 36, 40, 54).
   - It stays partial unless the "mark page non-writable" step is also applied on every CPU.
2. **Rows 25 + 48 + 20 (`4e3e72b1` + r6577 (e) + `6cb3514c`): set PFSR/PFAR on 88K bus errors.**
   - The guest's fault handler gets the status it reads; today it gets an exception with no status.
   - `lunadefaultstop`'s fault-based fix needs exactly this.
   - It also corrects our wrongly rejected triage record (finding 3).
3. **Row 57 (r6577 88K register-form `sub`, opcode 0x74).**
   - A legal 88100 instruction currently halts the emulator. The fix is a small decode entry plus a handler.
4. **Rows 27 + 24 + 22 (`a1cafe7c`, `922fc86b`, `8f4c717f`): mb89352.**
   - Decode SCMD by its top three bits, add the ATN commands, the BUS FREE interrupt and SDGC handling, and fix the bad format string.
   - This reduces how many guest actions can halt the CPU on the luna88k SCSI path we actually boot.
   - The halts themselves still need our own decline, because upstream relies on ERROR=halt.
5. **Row 53 (r6577 Alpha DEC 3000/300 `irq=` string).**
   - A one-token fix for a guaranteed SIGABRT when building a listed machine subtype (`ctorabortclass`).
   - Runner-up: the MXM warning instead of a halt (row 50, `m88kmxmkill`).

### A2: rpajarola/gxemul (gxemul-rp)

Source table: `A2_rpajarola.md`.

#### A2 — rpajarola/gxemul ("gxemul-rp") vs. the GXEMUL-SEC fork

Clone: `$S/clones/rpajarola__gxemul`. Scope reviewed: all 79 commits after the import on `master`
(`8911c8a..01299ad`, 78 plus 1 merge), the only branch-only commit (`668006d`, on
`claude/project-thread-1271qd`; `display-layer` and `machine-config` are fully merged), and the 6 own
commits on the 2015 0.6.x snapshot (`2da47ae..661900d`). Every diff was read; device and core hunks were
compared with the fork's function by reading it. Paths in Evidence are relative to `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`
unless noted; line numbers come from `grep -n` on the fork at its current working tree.

##### Baseline check (09d4f62 vs our pristine 0.7.0)

* `diff -rq $S/upstream070/src <09d4f62:src>` shows **one content difference**: `src/cpus/generate_arm_r.c`
  (3 lines). 09d4f62 has `(int)(1 << (c-1))`; ours has `(int)(1U << (c-1))` at :139/:200/:279. The other
  61 entries exist only in our baseline. They are generated files (`tmp_*.c`, `autodev.c`, `automachine.c`,
  `font8x*.c`, `ppc_spr_strings.h`) plus 2 stray `grep.exe.stackdump` files.
* **09d4f62 is the true tarball. Our "unmodified upstream baseline" is not.** 09d4f62's `src/` is
  byte-identical to the pristine 2021 copy `C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL_upstream_2021\root_src` for every
  file 09d4f62 has, and that copy also reads `1 <<`. So our `39748e3c` ("Import GXemul 0.7.0 (unmodified
  upstream baseline)") already carries fork fix **#13** (REVIEW_FINDINGS.md:46). Per that directory's
  README.txt, `gate_ab` builds its "pristine" tree from `git archive 39748e3`, so that tree includes #13.
  The effect is harmless (identical generator output), but the record is wrong.

##### Top picks

1. **Row 12 (fe69c7b): X11 cursor clipping.** A guest can trigger a host heap over-read or crash: on a
   PMAG-BA, any BT459 cursor Y of 865..960 passes dev_fb_setcursor's VRAM-height clamp. The fix is about
   20 self-contained lines in console/x11.c.
2. **Row 66 (e37235e): the RAM-shadowing hazard for sub-page MIPS devices** (KN02 RTC 0x100 bytes, DZ
   0x80 bytes in the 0x1fc00000 window). One guest store past the registers silently disconnects the
   device for dyntrans accesses. The fix is to register the whole page, or to fix the fallthrough in
   memory_rw.
3. **Row 16 (3e8f2fd): `debugger.o: debugger_cmds.c`.** This is a one-line Makefile.skel fix for a
   stale-binary hazard, measured with GNU Make 4.4.1. It bears directly on the fork's binary-freshness
   discipline.
4. **Row 53 (1c18c2b): NAT `SO_ERROR`/RST on a failed outgoing connect.** A small change in net_ip.c.
   Today a refused connection looks connected and then closes.
5. **Row 60 (807d80b): DC7085 CTS on lines 2/3.** A one-line fix for an OSF/1 console hang. Runners-up:
   row 61 (MODE SELECT block descriptor, keeping #133's bound), row 5 (raw-tape spurious zero record),
   row 56 (exact CD-ROM capacity), row 43 (R3010 FPU interrupt; larger).

### A3: jroark/GXemul (MIPS16, VR4131 / BE-300)

Source table: `A3_jroark.md`.

#### A3 -- jroark/GXemul against GXEMUL-SEC (HEAD de8c7547)

**Scope.** All 72 non-merge commits on `main`, `be300` and `claude/determined-cori-ew45xx` (the clone is not shallow; `be300` contains all of `cori`; `be300` restarts from the import with the squash [3c3d5cb](https://github.com/jroark/GXemul/commit/3c3d5cbfa23ce58718d6c1c348f44587b35c0911), so `main`'s MIPS16 series appears twice). Fork paths are `GXEMUL-SEC/src/...` at HEAD `de8c7547`. Read-only: nothing built or run. Every status comes from reading the function in our tree, and every line number from `grep -n` or a read of our file.

**Baseline check ([c35c056](https://github.com/jroark/GXemul/commit/c35c056440723f5e0e5b59f377ca5d409bcf963f)).** Its `src/` and `configure` are byte-identical to the April-2021 0.7.0 release tree (`GXEMUL_upstream_2021/archive_builds_20260625_203645/orig-asan`, mtimes 2021-04-22), apart from build products. Against `$S/upstream070` (our `39748e3c`) it differs in exactly two non-generated files: `configure` (39748e3c has the `-fgnu89-inline` probe, our #9) and `src/cpus/generate_arm_r.c` (39748e3c has `1U << (c-1)` where the release has `1 << (c-1)`, our #13). So `39748e3c`, though committed as the "unmodified upstream baseline", already carries fixes #9 and #13. The remaining differences are the expected generated files (`tmp_*.c`, `autodev.c`, `automachine.c`, `font8x*.c`, `ppc_spr_strings.h`) plus two `grep.exe.stackdump` files.

**MIPS16 in 0.7.0.** There is none. `cpu_mips_coproc.c:61` has `const int m16 = 0; /* TODO: MIPS16 support */` (same in our tree), `opcodes_mips.h:428` has `/* JALX (TODO) 0x1d */`, and the only MIPS16 artefact is the test ELF `test/FileLoader_ELF_MIPS16`. So every MIPS16 interpreter commit is FEATURE. Where one removes a guest-reachable halt in shared code, that part is split out below (JALX, row 13).

**VR41xx sources.** Our `dev_vr41xx.c` and `dev_ns16550.c` are byte-identical to 0.7.0 (`cmp`). No VR41xx manual is in `_scratchpad/`; our own record `wdchpcmipsaddr` says so. VR4131 offsets and semantics below are therefore jroark's, taken from their code, commit messages and VR4131 UM citations.

##### Top picks

1. **Row 13: JALX and the other unassigned primary opcodes stop the emulator** (`cpu_mips_instr.c:5708`). Any guest code, including user mode, can end the emulation. The fix is the `instr(reserved)` idiom #236/#237 already use (CpU for the COP2 forms), and it is unfiled.
2. **Row 26: cap `compare_interrupts_pending`.** A one-line cap ends the IP7 burst after a long masked period, and removes the uncapped-`int` shape #442 fixed in `dev_footbridge.c`. The host-timer path is live on algor/evbmips; the backlog half is filed as `dfreq`.
3. **Row 28: raise IP7 when an MFC0 Count bump reaches Compare.** About ten lines in `coproc_register_read()`. It removes a guest-visible "Count past Compare but IP7 clear" state that Linux's `c0_compare_int_usable()` probe checks; unfiled.
4. **Row 34: report ns16550 IIR as a prioritised ID.** One UART model shared by many machines. It currently misreports RX-plus-THRE as Receiver Line Status (0x6) and loses THRE on unrelated IIR reads; unfiled.
5. **Row 32: R4100 Context.BadVPN2 = VA[31:11].** jroark's two-line change ports directly to `cpu_mips.c:2078-2088` and is the R4100 half of the filed MED row `mipsbadvpnscale`.

### A4: prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860 and forks, PowerPC CPU

Source table: `A4_rs6000_ppc.md`.

#### A4: PowerPC CPU fixes in prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860, checked against GXEMUL-SEC

**Scope and method**
- Base: the fork's first commit 9dba949 matches upstream SVN r5846 (retroprom `8ec5d380`, 2014-08-18) byte-for-byte in 11 of the 12 PPC/CPU files. The only difference is in `cpu_ppc.cc`, where the MSR[IP] reboot hack is exempted for the IBM 860.
- Between that base and upstream 0.7.0, the PPC CPU files differ only in the FP NaN plumbing, the openfirmware stop, `vaddr_mask` and the page-cross `exit` becoming `running=false`. So the base defects listed here are also in 0.7.0 unless a row says otherwise.
- Commits read: 197 commits on 44 branches touch the CPU paths, and 150 of them touch the PPC-specific files.
  - `b31c0ea` squashes the 2023-06..2025-10 branch line into main. Its PPC files are tree-identical to `origin/20250408-tlb`.
  - Branch-only PPC content not in main exists only in `59cc410` (os/2 branch, merged into the pcmcia branch by `2765b8f`). Its one fix is the tlbie change in row 21.
  - The four forks (Cacodemon345, GenSayer, kristibektashi, WNT50) carry no PPC-CPU commits beyond origin. Their only extra commit is merge `7413915`, which has no PPC change.
- Citation conventions:
  - Our paths are under `GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/` unless prefixed. Line numbers are from HEAD `de8c7547`; the PPC files are clean in the working tree.
  - "Book I" = `_scratchpad/ppc_uisa_book1_v201.txt`. "PEM32" = `_scratchpad/ppc_pem32_rev2_2001.txt`.

##### Top picks

1. **Undecoded legal instructions that stop emulation (rows 7-11):** twi/tw, lswx/stswx, crnand, the OE=1 add/subf forms plus `addc.`, and dcbi. Each is cheap decoder work, the fork's handlers are a reference once its slips are fixed, and Linux/ppc's MMIO read accessors issue `twi 0,rX,0`.
2. **crandc/crorc compute NAND/NOR (rows 14-15):** these are silent wrong condition results in compiled code. Each fix is one line, Book I `:1903-1914` is unambiguous, and no record of the defect exists.
3. **slw/srw/sraw shift amounts 32-63 (row 16, filed HIGH as `ppcshiftwide` with a rung-3 witness):** the fork's slw/srw masks drop straight in, though sraw still needs its own fix.
4. **Interrupt entry state (rows 18, 19 and 22, ported together):** clear IR/DR/FP/SE/BE/FE0/FE1/POW, set LE from ILE, set the ISI reasons in SRR1, and flush the EA-keyed translation cache when IR/DR change. Our NetBSD rigs only work because of a 1:1 BAT coincidence.
5. **`qhslowifetch` (row 1, filed HIGH):** this is a host `exit(1)` on a guest instruction fetch. The fork's fix is small: return after the already-raised ISI.

### A5: prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860 and forks, devices, disk, console, core

Source table: `A5_rs6000_devices.md`.

#### A5 - prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860 (non-PowerPC) vs GXEMUL-SEC

Base of the fork = upstream SVN r5845 (retroprom `865050cb`, 2014-08-18); first fork commit `9dba949` = base + IBM NVRAM/ROM/passthrough edits. Scope: 448 non-merge commits on all 44 origin branches plus forks Cacodemon345, GenSayer, kristibektashi, WNT50 (only one fork-only commit: Cacodemon345 merge `7413915`, dev_86mc65 resolution). 286 commits touch files that also exist in 0.7.0; the PPC CPU files were excluded. Our-fork line numbers are from `GXEMUL-SEC/src` read on 2026-10-22.

Fix guards `d->divisor ? ... : 0`. | CRASH/BOUNDS | STILL OPEN (filed as `ns16550`; row closed CLOSE(assess) and carried by open `closeassessrows`) | ours `devices/dev_ns16550.c:179-181` unchanged; OUTSTANDING_BUGS "ns16550: the divide-by-zero is REAL but DOES NOT FIRE IN THE SHIPPED BUILD" (SIGFPE at -O0/-O1, elided at -O2+). |

##### Top picks (STILL OPEN, most valuable to port)

- [8bcf95a](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/8bcf95a20ad8285503b81a2796f8430dd513cf10) - ns16550 divisor guard: one token removes a guest-reachable divide-by-zero that kills the host at -O0/-O1 (`ns16550`, carried by `closeassessrows`).
- [598ab10](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/598ab108cacb56dfbeae33eb463fd2b8c18393dd) - 8259 OCW2/OCW3 decode without `cpu->running = 0`: any guest PIC command outside the short list halts emulation today (`8259cmdhalt`); take the command decode, not the PReP-specific `assert(addr > 0x808680000000)`.
- [ae2240b](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/ae2240b30a994913d56f904533ee64d0178682ee) - 8259 EOI must clear ISR only: specific EOI wipes the IRR bit of a still-asserted line (`dev_8259.c:122`), a two-line change.
- [eb4c571](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/eb4c571747357b394ec15fc2b9f9a31efb999ef5) - Eagle sub-dword config access: byte/word reads at 0xCFD-0xCFF return 0 on prep/mvmeppc, so drivers reading INTERRUPT_PIN/LINE or STATUS bytes see nothing.
- [d2ff00e](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/d2ff00eb9d9dc8e8b660672c9a155a8131cb2b20), [c861ff6](https://github.com/prozacchiwawa/gxemul-rs6000-860/commit/c861ff6d1909a97715eddf7d30b8d8cabd352519) - pckbc Set-LEDs parameter: any non-zero LED byte (Caps/Num Lock) is NAKed with RESEND today; consume and ACK it and the `case 0x00` workaround becomes unnecessary.

### A6: CTSRD-CHERI family and ryoon/gxemul (pkgsrc patches)

Source table: `A6_cheri_pkgsrc.md`.

#### A6 -- CTSRD-CHERI family + ryoon/gxemul (pkgsrc) vs GXEMUL-SEC

All `file:line` citations without a prefix are in `GXEMUL-SEC/src/` (read 2026-10-22, working tree). `U070:` = `$S/upstream070/src/`. `code2:` = upstream SVN mirror `$S/clones/ryoon__gxemul-code2`. Manuals = `_scratchpad/*.txt`. Nothing was built or run.

Mirror check: SivilTaram, staceyson, seanpm2001 carry 0 commits outside origin's four branches (`git rev-list --count ... --not origin/*`).

##### (a) CTSRD-CHERI family

| # | Source commit URL | Files / function | Description (1-2 sentences) | Category | Status in our fork | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul/commit/d50388f8976eb811f839b9302b3894d19b7f5621 | cpu_mips_coproc.cc `fpu_op()` FPU_OP_C; float_emul.cc | Rewrites all 16 c.cond.fmt predicates. In 0.6.0, c.olt/c.ole were true for any ordered pair (`OR !unordered`) and 9 conditions were `#if 0` and hit fatal(). It also widens the S NaN test to "exponent all-ones", which wrongly includes +/-Inf until bc20f00. | CORRECTNESS | ALREADY FIXED | 0.7.0 still has the broken switch (`U070:cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:1208-1245`). Ours uses the cond-bit truth table `(cond&4 AND less) OR (cond&2 AND equal) OR (cond&1 AND unordered)` at `cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:1441-1461`, CHANGELOG round 28 `#254`. It agrees with CHERI's table on all 16 conds. Neither version raises V on signaling predicates; that gap is filed as `qhfcsrflags`. |
| 2 | https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul/commit/4c6eedc33e57f7fa3cd7b4130c71f26562a0edca | cpu_mips_coproc.cc `fpu_function()`/`fpu_op()` | Decodes round/ceil/floor.{w,l} and turns trunc.{w,l} into a real truncate, using host `round()`/`ceil()`/`floor()`/`trunc()`. | CORRECTNESS | ALREADY FIXED | 0.7.0 still lacked them (`U070:...coproc.c:1035` TODO list). An unmatched function hit fatal() plus CpU(1). Ours: trunc forces RZ at `cpu_mips_coproc.c:1613-1638` (`#294`). round/ceil/floor force RN/RP/RM at `:1669-1708` (`#295`). CHERI's code is wrong on ties: C `round()` rounds half away from zero, but ROUND.W.fmt is "rounding to nearest/even (rounding mode 0)" (`mips64_volII.txt:1421`). Ours rounds ties to even (`core/float_emul.c:322-324`). Do not port. |
| 3 | https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul/commit/bc20f00f6b11e8318f1a929eef6f70f9d1ff42a5 | float_emul.cc `ieee_interpret_float_value()` | Replaces 0.6.0's exact-bit-pattern NaN test (`x == 0x7fffffff` ...) with "exponent all-ones AND fraction != 0" for S and D. | CORRECTNESS | NOT PRESENT | Upstream fixed this before 0.7.0: Inf is tested first, then exponent all-ones means NaN (`U070:core/float_emul.c:86-105`). Ours is identical at `core/float_emul.c:128-149` (Inf at `:133`/`:142`, NaN at `:137`/`:146`). |
| 4 | https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul/commit/1236c18954d858650dcc6267ede063fad1e6df6b (= PR #1) | cpu_mips_instr.cc `X(div)`, `X(ddiv)` | Guards the INT_MIN / -1 operand pair, which raises a host SIGFPE on x86. (Part a: the host crash.) | CRASH/BOUNDS | NOT PRESENT | Upstream added its own guard from gson's pkgsrc patch (`code2:dadcc607`, 2018-08-19). Ours: `cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:1839` (div) and `:1863` (ddiv). |
| 5 | same 1236c18 | `X(div)`, `X(ddiv)` | Part b, the result value. CHERI returns LO=a, HI=0; upstream and ours return LO=0. | CORRECTNESS | STILL OPEN (unfiled) | Ours: div gives LO=0, HI=0 (`cpu_mips_instr.c:1839-1840`). ddiv gives LO=0 and HI=a, because `rem = a - b*0` (`:1863-1867`). The manuals mark only divisor-zero as UNPREDICTABLE: MIPS64 DIV `mips64_volII.txt:606` ("LO <- sign_extend(q31..0)", "No arithmetic exception occurs under any circumstances"), DDIV `:535`, R4000 `mips_r4000.txt:4721`/`:4738`. So the specified result is the wrapped quotient (LO=0xffffffff80000000 / 0x8000000000000000) with HI=0, which is CHERI's answer. Our own m88k `#195` (`cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:732-737`) already chose the wrapped 0x80000000. No regress row pins LO=0. |
| 6 | https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul/commit/b41a152911f80ef22755bd7ce6e09638318551a4 | cpu_mips_instr.cc `to_be_translated` HI6_COP2 | Treats every COP2 instruction as a NOP. CheriBSD's capability ops re-trapped CpU(2) forever. | CORRECTNESS (CHERI-specific) | NOT PRESENT | Ours = 0.7.0: COP2 with `coproc[2]==NULL` goes to `instr(cpu)`, i.e. CpU with CE=2 (`cpu_mips_instr.c:5123-5133`; `X(cpu)` at `:148-157`). Only coproc[0] and [1] are ever allocated (`cpus/cpu_mips.c:262-263`). The emulator cannot loop by itself. A guest-side loop needs a handler that sets Status.CU2 and retries; X(cpu) ignores CU2 (the same shape `#295` recorded for CU1). CpU(2) is the architected answer for a COP2-less CPU (`mips_r4000.txt:1158`), so NOP-ing would be wrong. Adjacent issue, not this commit: LWC2/LDC2/SWC2/SDC2 halt emulation (see note A2). |
| 7 | https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul/commit/cc620bbbcfec8703bbfa9194af48e932aad7689f | cpu_mips_instr.cc `X(ddiv)` | Adds an LL suffix to CHERI's own 64-bit literals so 32-bit hosts compile. | BUILD | n/a | Ours uses `ULL` (`cpu_mips_instr.c:1863`). |
| 8 | https://github.com/CTSRD-CHERI/gxemul/commit/f5ee704574a5801c73199eeef7da01c9008ce67e | cpu_dyntrans.cc, debugger.cc, main.cc | New `-u` option: deliver interrupts while single-stepping (needed by the BERI test suite). | FEATURE | n/a | Absent from ours. The guard is still `if (!single_step)` (`cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c:204-206`), a debugger design choice. |

##### Top picks (STILL OPEN, most valuable to port)

1. **RECIP.fmt / RSQRT.fmt (+ MOVCF/MOVZ/MOVN.fmt) decode** (row 15). Valid MIPS IV/MIPS32-64 FP instructions currently cause an ungated fatal() and an endless CpU(1) re-trap (`cpu_mips_coproc.c:2585-2589`). This is the `#295` defect class, reachable on every FPU MIPS IV/64 CPU. Write our own decoder; TheFausap's code is dead.
2. **MIPS DIV/DDIV INT_MIN/-1 result** (row 5). It's a one-line change per op to match the manual (LO = wrapped quotient, HI = 0, i.e. CHERI's answer), consistent with our m88k `#195`. Today ddiv even returns HI = a.
3. **Footbridge SDRAM_BA_MASK/OFFSET read `exit(1)`** (row 28, `fbsdrampair`). A guest-reachable host kill that this derivative introduced and upstream absorbed. Nothing to port; fix it locally (already filed HIGH).
4. **SH user-mode access to P1/P2/P4 must be a Data Address Error, not a TLB miss** (row 33, `shuseraddr`). Neither pkgsrc's TLB_PROT nor upstream's TLB_MISS is right; the manual is explicit (EXPEVT H'0E0/H'100, VBR+0x100).
5. **SCIF FDR receive count clamp 255 -> 16** (row 30, `qhscifssr`). A one-token fix to the 5-bit field per SH7750 section 16.2.10.

##### Adjacent observations (not fixes in these derivatives; found while reading; unfiled per grep of OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md / ledger.json)

- **A1** VR41xx: `coproc_tlbwri()` still `fatal()+exit(1)`s when a TLB write has a valid entry with PageMask 0. That is a 1 KB page, legal on VR41xx, and R4100 PageMask writes skip `#188`'s canonicalisation (`cpu_mips_coproc.c:748-750`). Sites: `:2037-2042` (old entry) and `:2165-2170` (new entry). A guest-reachable host kill on hpcmips.
- **A2** LWC2/LDC2/SWC2/SDC2/SWC3 (opcodes 0x32/0x36/0x3a/0x3e/0x3b) have no case. They hit `default: goto bad` (`cpu_mips_instr.c:5708`), which stops emulation (`cpu_dyntrans.c:1983`, `:2014` `cpu->running = 0`) instead of raising CpU(2)/CpU(3).
- **A3** MIPS IV/MIPS64 COP1X (opcode 0x13: MADD/MSUB/NMADD/NMSUB.fmt, LWXC1/LDXC1/SWXC1/SDXC1, PREFX) always raises CpU(3) (`cpu_mips_instr.c:5136-5142`). SPECIAL MOVCI (movf/movt, func 0x01; not even defined at `include/opcodes_mips.h:170`) falls to `goto bad` (`cpu_mips_instr.c:4679`).

### A7: smaller and older repositories, and the ajfa recipes

Source table: `A7_small_repos.md`.

#### A7: small and older GXemul repositories, checked against the hardened fork

Scope: the repositories assigned to A7. Method: I diffed every commit each repo made itself, using `git show`, or a tree diff
against the matching upstream SVN commit in `clones/retroprom__gxemul`. I decided each status by reading the function in
`GXEMUL-SEC/src/`. Line numbers come from `grep -n` on the GXEMUL-SEC working tree on 2026-10-22. Nothing was built or run.
Paths in the Evidence column are relative to `GXEMUL-SEC/`.

##### 1. Repositories

The import tree equals upstream SVN a12c4242 plus generated Makefiles/config.h | 6 | HUST HOS-MIPS adaptations. The R6000 entry is repurposed as a MIPS32r2 core, with EBase, and an unknown primary opcode raises RI |

Turns the R6000 entry into ISA 32 rev 2 and adds a debug print. | FEATURE | n/a | HOS-specific. Our R6000 entry is upstream's (`src/include/mips_cpu_types.h:72`). |
| 2 | https://github.com/klx3300/gxemul-hos/commit/8f94a57900ee6385d6fb6fcd09f9320af12a95c2 | cpu_mips_coproc.cc `coproc_register_write` (PRID sel 1), cpu_mips.cc `mips_cpu_exception` | Implements EBase (CP0 15 sel 1). A write stores it, and the non-BEV exception base becomes EBase instead of 0x80000000. | CORRECTNESS | STILL OPEN (unfiled) | We model Release-2 cores: 4KEc, 5KE and 24KEc (`src/include/mips_cpu_types.h:102,104,105`). `mtc0 $15,1` hits `COP0_PRID: readonly = 1` (`src/cpus/cpu_mips_coproc.c:892-894`). It is dropped with an unlatched `fatal()` (:1011-1014). `mfc0 $15,1` returns PRId, because the select is ignored (:601, :647). The vector base is hard-wired (`src/cpus/cpu_mips.c:2165-2168`). No record mentions EBase. |
| 3 | https://github.com/klx3300/gxemul-hos/commit/366d5cc51b807b311a3bb26e07851b42e651806f | cpu_mips_instr.cc `to_be_translated`, top-level opcode switch | `default: goto bad` becomes `ic->f = instr(reserved)`: an undecoded primary opcode raises RI instead of stopping the machine. | CORRECTNESS (a user-mode instruction stops the emulator) | STILL OPEN (unfiled) | Our code still has `default:goto bad;` (`src/cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:5708`), and `bad:` sets `cpu->running = 0` (`src/cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c:2014`). Opcodes with no case arm: 0x1d JALX, 0x32 LWC2, 0x36 LDC2, 0x3a SWC2, 0x3b SWC3, 0x3e SDC2 (`src/include/opcodes_mips.h:428-479`). Same shape nearby: LWC3 below ISA 4 (:5552-5560) and 0x1e on non-R5900 (:5564-5574). `mipscop0tail` covers only the COP0 arms. Upstream SVN head still says `default:goto bad`. Architecturally LWC2/SWC2/LDC2/SDC2 should raise CpU, as our HI6_COP2 arm already does (:5123-5129), and JALX/0x1e should raise RI. hos uses RI for all of them. |

The `#ifdef` merge is the fork's own regression: the compiler ignores the extra tokens, so the delay-slot guard disappears. Ours keeps the guard (`src/cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c:1890-1892`). |

| 24 | https://github.com/ajfa/mvme187-sysv88-gxemul/commit/4a01173afc4beafabbd61ca385bd158d07d4a415 (patch 01) | cpu_m88k.c `m88k_stcr` PSR/EPSR | 88100 PSR bits 27..4 are reserved, so writes are masked with 0xf000000f. SVR4 ORs its IPL into saved PSR copies; with junk left in those bits, every process resumed at spl7. | CORRECTNESS | STILL OPEN (unfiled) | PSR and EPSR are stored verbatim (`src/cpus/cpu_m88k.c:553,557`), and `rte` copies EPSR into PSR through that store (`src/cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:2249`). The manual: "Only bits 31-28 and 3-0 can be modified by an stcr or xcr" (`_scratchpad/mc88100.txt:1979`); reserved bits read 0 and writes are ignored (:1899-1900, :2024). Bits 9-4 are hard-wired to one (:2031); neither our reset value (`cpu_m88k.c:181`) nor the recipe's mask models that. |
| 25 | same, patch 01 | cpu_m88k_instr.c register-op decode | Adds register-form `sub` (0x74, signed, traps on overflow). | CORRECTNESS (a legal 88100 instruction stops the CPU) | STILL OPEN (unfiled) | The case list covers 0x60-0x67, 0x70, 0x78 and 0x7c, but not 0x71-0x77 (`src/cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:3660-3685`), so they fall to `default:goto bad` (:3815). Affected: `sub`, `sub.co/.ci/.cio` and `add.co/.ci/.cio` (`mc88100.txt:3844`, :6025-6028). This includes the manual's own "set carry" idiom `sub.co r0,r0,r0` (:6047-6048). The recipe adds only 0x74. |

The recipe computes in host double; ours keeps the #298/#299 rounding. |

| 28 | same, patch 01 | cpu_m88k_instr.c xmem decode | Instruction word 0x00000000 (`xmem.bu r0,r0,0`) no longer goes to `bad`; SVR4's IPL runs through zero padding. | CORRECTNESS (CPU stop) | STILL OPEN (unfiled) | `if (iword == 0) goto bad;` (`src/cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:3047-3048`). The immediate xmem encoding is at `mc88100.txt:6413, 6478-6482`. Upstream, the recipe and ours all turn an xmem with rD=r0 into a NOP (:3045-3046, :3657-3658), which drops the store half of the exchange. |

The decoder indexes those entries (`cpu_m88k_instr.c:3608-3609, 3622-3629`), which leads to "INTERNAL ERROR: ic->f == NULL!" and a CPU stop (`src/cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c:1906-1908`). The manual defines `st.b rD,rS1[rS2]` with scale 1 (`mc88100.txt:5893, 5933`). |

It indexes `m88k_loadstore[]` with SCALEDNESS (:2377-2382); generated entry 432 is NULL. It then calls `xmem_load(cpu,&call)` at :2395. The manual defines `xmem.bu rD,rS1[rS2]` with scale 1 (`mc88100.txt:6423, 6459`). The code is unchanged from 39748e3c. |

TODO"); goto bad; }` (`src/cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:3606-3614`). The 88100 encoding diagram shows bit 7 as 0 (`mc88100.txt:5944-5986`). The `exit(1)`s are at :80 (memconf write) and :90 (default). The recipe's `if (relative_addr < sizeof(d->memcreg))` is the bound the row asks for. It also shows 187Bug itself reaches the :80 exit. |
| 50 | same, patch 07 | new dev_i82596.c; dev_pcc2.c IE line; net.c `GXEMUL_NAMESERVER`; net_ip.c DNS PTR synthesis | An 82596CA Ethernet device for SVR4. | FEATURE | n/a | We map 0xfff46000 as `unreadable` (`src/machines/machine_mvme88k.c:106`). Caveats if ported: `malloc(2048)` is unchecked, and the TBD/RBD walks have no iteration cap, so a zero-length self-linked descriptor loops forever on the host. |
| 51 | same, patch 08 | machine_mvme88k.c | Maps the 187Bug EPROM and SRAM under `-Q`; sets r3/r7 to the boot device. | FEATURE | n/a | The r3/r7 TODO is still at `src/machines/machine_mvme88k.c:169-170`. |
| 52 | https://github.com/ajfa/mach3-cmu-pmax/commit/5cfc043186490b2cbd8670c0995d02862d36e4ce (`patches/gxemul-dc7085-mach3.patch`) | dev_dc7085.c `DEVICE_TICK(dc7085)` | Removes `if (TRDY) return;`, so receive (RDONE) is still processed while the transmitter is ready. Mach 3.0 keyboard input was ignored. | CORRECTNESS | STILL OPEN (unfiled) | `src/devices/dev_dc7085.c:149-150`. The tick runs after every register access (:285-286), and TCR/TDR writes clear TRDY (:238, :257), so a driver that writes TCR/TDR on every pass never sees RDONE. The recipe's own explanation of the mechanism ("leaves TRDY set") does not match this code path; the fix is the recipe's measurement. Caveat: the in-code comment keeps the return for Ultrix, so Ultrix and NetBSD/pmax must be re-checked. |
| 53 | same repo, README (5cfc043) | cpu_mips_instr.c SPECIAL decode | SYNC (funct 0x0f) is not MIPS I: an R3000 must raise RI. Mach 3.0's user emulator uses `op_tas = 0x0000000f`, so under GXemul it spins forever. | CORRECTNESS | STILL OPEN (unfiled) | `case SPECIAL_SYNC: ic->f = instr(nop);` with no ISA gate (`src/cpus/cpu_mips_instr.c:4675-4677`). Our header marks SYNC as MIPS II (`src/include/opcodes_mips.h:184`). |
| 54 | same repo, README | R3000 cache sizing | MK83's cache-sizing routine "jumps to address zero" in GXemul; the workaround patches 4 kernel words. | CORRECTNESS | UNCERTAIN | We model Status.IsC (`src/cpus/memory_mips.c:84-110`), but SwC is only displayed (`src/cpus/cpu_mips.c:1607`). Settle it with an MK83 boot or by reading its sizing routine. |
| 55 | same repo, README (ec96a17) | host console | With stdout redirected to a file, GXemul prints nothing and hangs, so the recipe uses a pty (`script -qc`). | OTHER | n/a | Host I/O behaviour, not emulation. |

##### 3. Top picks

1. **#30, scaled `xmem.bu rD,rS1[rS2]`:** a user process can make the host call a NULL function pointer. The fix is one line: treat scaled byte as unscaled, as the recipe does for ld/st, but without its `op != 3` exemption.
2. **#3, the MIPS primary-opcode `default: goto bad`:** a user-mode JALX/LWC2/SWC2/LDC2/SDC2/SWC3 stops pmax/arc emulation. This is the same shape #309 fixed for REGIMM. Use CpU for the coprocessor-2/3 opcodes and RI for the rest.
3. **#49, `mvme187memcoob`:** the recipe supplies exactly the bound the filed row asks for. It also shows 187Bug writes memconf, which reaches the `exit(1)` at `dev_mvme187.c:80`.
4. **#25/#28/#29/#31, m88k decode stops on legal 88100 encodings:** `sub`/`add.c*`/`sub.c*` (including the manual's `sub.co r0,r0,r0` idiom), scaled `ld.b`/`st.b`, `.wt` and 0x00000000. Each is cited from the manual and measured by the recipe under SVR4.
5. **#24, m88k PSR/EPSR reserved bits:** the manual says writes to bits 27..4 are ignored; it is a two-line mask, and SVR4 depends on it (otherwise it returns at spl7).

##### 4. Notes for the coordinator

- **The "pristine 0.7.0" reference used by this research is not pristine.** Fork commit 39748e3c ("Import GXemul 0.7.0 (unmodified upstream baseline) ... Imported verbatim") and `$S/upstream070/` both differ in content from real 0.7.0 in two files. Real 0.7.0 here means upstream SVN 743170f0, which is identical to Debian's 0.7.0 tarball import e4b5b4d in threader/gxemul and to eddieh's base.
  - `configure` already contains the fork's `-fgnu89-inline` block (REVIEW_FINDINGS #9).
  - `src/cpus/generate_arm_r.c` already has `1U << (c-1)` (REVIEW_FINDINGS #13).
  - It also contains build products (`src/cpus/tmp_*.c`, `autodev.c`, fonts, `automachine.c`, `ppc_spr_strings.h`, two `grep.exe.stackdump`), and it lacks some test and Makefile files.

  Any agent that compares against 39748e3c would class #9- and #13-type fixes as "NOT PRESENT (fixed upstream)". In this fork's terms, the commit message is a wrong record.
- **Device-behaviour claims in the MVME187 recipe that bear on open rows:**
  - 187Bug writes MEMC memconf (#49; `mvme187memcoob` and `exitunowned76`).
  - 187Bug expects VMEchip2 registers it does not know to read back what was written (#46, against #568's decline).
  - The PCC2 "value kept" policy differs from #501's decline (#44).
  - 187Bug's board-data checksum sits at NVRAM 0x1ff0 and is written with 16/32-bit accesses (#47; supports #521's reading).
  - SCRIPTS run from on-board SRAM and from RAM through the bus (#36; `osiopzeroram`).
  - SVR4 executes the SCRIPTS features that still `exit(1)` (#38; `exitunowned76`).

  All of these are claims by the recipe's author, measured with a ROM and a disk image that are not in our tree.
